### Q1. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A cyclic process for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of an ideal gas is shown in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi></math> diagram. The work done in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>BC</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CA</mi></math> respectively are

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0016904_Question_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 169px; height: 146px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>0,</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"/><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>ln</mtext><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mtext>,</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"/><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>T</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mtext>,&#160;</mtext><mfenced><mrow><mi>T</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mtext>,</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"/><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>ln</mtext><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>0,</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>ln</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>,</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow>  <mtext>0,</mtext>  <mspace width="0.5em" />  <msub>          <mtext  mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext>        <mn>2</mn>  </msub>  <mtext>ln</mtext>  <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">    <mrow>      <mfrac>        <mrow>          <msub>                <mrow>                  <mi>V</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>                </mrow>                <mn>1</mn>          </msub>        </mrow>        <mrow>                      <mrow>              <msub>                    <mrow>                      <mi>V</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>                    </mrow>                    <mn>2</mn>              </msub>            </mrow>                  </mrow>      </mfrac>    </mrow>  </mfenced>  <mi>,</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>  <mi>R</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>  <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">    <mrow>      <msub>            <mrow>              <mi>T</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>            </mrow>            <mn>1</mn>      </msub>      <mo>-</mo>      <msub>            <mrow>              <mi>T</mi><mo>&ApplyFunction;</mo>            </mrow>            <mn>2</mn>      </msub>    </mrow>  </mfenced></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mtext>0,</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>ln</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>,</mi><mo>⁡</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

During AB process is isochoric.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

During <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BC</mi></math>, the process is isothermal.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0016904_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 169px; height: 146px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mspace width="0.5em"/><mspace width="0.5em"/><mi>W</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mtext>ln</mtext><mfrac><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

During <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CA</mi></math>, the process is isobaric. So, the pressure is constant.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8211;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math>

But <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>W</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>V</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q2. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A monoatomic gas undergoes a process in which the pressure <math><mo>(</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and the volume <math><mo>(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo></math> of the gas are related as <math><mi>P</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant. What will be the molar heat capacity of gas for this process?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>P</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>t</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math>  

 <math><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  

 <math><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  

 <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  

 <math><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>

</details>

### Q3. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two rigid boxes containing different ideal gases are placed on a table. Box <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> contains one mole of nitrogen at temperature <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>, while box <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> contains one mole of helium at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>. The boxes are then put into thermal contact with each other, and heat flows between them until the gases reach a common final temperature (Ignore the heat capacity of boxes). Then , the final temperature of the gases, <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo></math> in terms of <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, change in internal energy of the system is zero, ie, increase in internal energy of one is equal to decrease in internal energy of other.

<b><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029171_Ans.jpg" style="width: 250px; height: 105px;" /></b>

<math><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&times;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></math>

<math><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&times;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></math>

Now, <math><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mn>5</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mo>&rArr;</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>8</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>&rArr;</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

</details>

### Q4. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A liquid of density <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> flows through a calorimeter at the rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Heat is added by means of a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> electric heating coil and a temperature difference of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>°</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is established in steady-state conditions between the inflow and the outflow points of the liquid. The specific heat for the liquid will be (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4186</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> )

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#xA0;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>This is a problem on &#39;flow calorimeter&#39; used to measure the specific heat of the liquid.
Amount of heat supplied to the water per second by the heating coil = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>   <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mn>8</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kcal</mtext> </mrow> </math>

The volume of liquid flowing out per second = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cm</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p>Mass of this liquid = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>

The temperature rise of this mass of liquid = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math></p><p>Hence,  <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mn>8</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mst</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>0.85</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>8</mn> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mtext>s</mtext> <mo>&#215;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </math>

Hence,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>s</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>0.85</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.6&#160;</mtext><mi>kcal</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mo>K</mo><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q5. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The pressure <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>, volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> and temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> of a certain material are related by the equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>α</mi><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>V</mi></math>. The work done by the material at constant pressure, when the temperature changes from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>&#x3B1;</mi><msubsup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>&#x3B1;</mi><msubsup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>&#x3B1;</mi><msubsup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mi>&#x3B1;</mi><msubsup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>&#x3B1;</mi><msubsup><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given relation is

<math> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>α</mi> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>∴</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>V</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>α</mi> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

As pressure is kept constant, <math> <mrow> <mtext>dV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>α</mi> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>dT</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>W</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>∫</mo> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>·</mo> <mtext>dV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <munderover> <mo>∫</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>2T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </munderover> <mtext>P</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>α</mi> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>dT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>α</mi> <msubsup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>2T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>α</mi> <msubsup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q6. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The figure shows two isotherms at temperatures <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. A gas is taken from one isotherm to another isotherm through different processes. Then change in internal energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>Δ</mo><mtext>U</mtext></math> has a relation –

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032756.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 190px; height: 132px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ac</mtext> </msub> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ad</mtext> </msub> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ae</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ac</mtext> </msub> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ad</mtext> </msub> <mo>&gt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ae</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ac</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ad</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ae</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>&lt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ac</mtext> </msub> <mo>&lt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ad</mtext> </msub> <mo>&lt;</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ae</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ab</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ac</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ad</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <msub> <mtext>U</mtext> <mtext>ae</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Internal energy depends only on state but not on the process. Hence its change is same for all the processes between two same states.

</details>

### Q7. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal gas as taken from state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to state <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> by three different processes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mi>ACB</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mi>ADB</mi><mo> </mo></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mi>AEB</mi></math> as shown in the<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram. The heat absorbed by the gas is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029740_Question.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 152px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Greater in process (b) than in (a)
- **(B)** The least in process (b)
- **(C)** The same in (a) and (c)
- **(D)** Less in (c) than in (b)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Less in (c) than in (b)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Heat absorbed by gas in three processes is given by

             <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mtext>ACB</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">W</mtext><mtext>ACB</mtext></msub></math>

             <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mtext>ADB</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext></math>

             <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mtext>AEB</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">W</mtext><mtext>AEB</mtext></msub></math>

The change in internal energy in all the three cases is the same and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>ACB</mi></msub></math> is positive, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>AEB</mi></msub></math> is negative.

Hence       <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mtext>ACB</mtext></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mtext>ADB</mtext></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">Q</mtext><mtext>AEB</mtext></msub></math>

</details>

---

### Q8. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram of a diatomic gas is a straight line parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>-axis. The molar heat capacity of the gas in the process will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The process described is isochoric

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> molar heat capacity <math><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2.5</mn><mi>R</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q9. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A work of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>146</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi></math> is performed on the gas, in order to compress one kilomole of gas adiabatically and in this process the temperature of the gas increases by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The gas is ( <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

**Options:**

- **(A)** monoatomic
- **(B)** diatomic
- **(C)** triatomic
- **(D)** a mixture of monoatomic and diatomic

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) diatomic**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>According to first law of thermodynamics

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>Q</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>U</mtext><mo>+</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>W</mtext></math>

For an adiabatic process, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>Q=0</mtext></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>U</mtext><mo>+</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>W</mtext></math>

or  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>U</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>W</mtext></math>

or  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><msub><mtext>C</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></msub><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>T</mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>W</mtext></math>

or  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>C</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>W</mtext></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#916;</mo><mtext>T</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mtext>146</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mtext>10</mtext><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mtext>10</mtext><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

For diatomic gas,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext>C</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext>R</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mtext>8.3&#160;=&#160;20.8&#160;J&#160;mol</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mtext>&#160;K</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Hence the gas is diatomic.</p>

</details>

---

### Q10. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Two moles of an ideal monoatomic gas is<span style="font-size: 10.8333px;"> </span>taken through a cycle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> as shown in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi></math> diagram. During the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math>, the pressure and temperature of the gas vary such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mi>T</mi></math> = constant. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> and the amount of heat released by the gas during the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mi>R</mi></math>, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0007626_Ques1_1.jpg" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2100**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mi>γ</mi></msup></math> = constant

Molar heat capacity, <math><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mi>R</mi><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mi>R</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math></p><p>Here the process is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>V</mi></math> = constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math><sup> </sup>= constant</p><p>i.e, <math><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>
 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mtext>.</mtext><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>Δ</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi><mo>×</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>300</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>600</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
 <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2100</mn><mi>R</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q11. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A body takes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>min</mi></math> to cool from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">℃</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">℃</mi></math>. If the temperature of the surroundings is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">℃</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>527</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">℃</mi></math> respectively. The final temperature of the body is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>48</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>46</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>49</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>42.85</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>42.85</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Newton's law

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> ....(i)

Let <math><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math> be the temperature after another 10 min

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> ....(ii)

Dividing Eq.(i) by Eq. (ii), we get

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>30</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>42.85 </mn><mi mathvariant="normal">℃</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q12. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The internal energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> mol of hydrogen of temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> is equal to the internal energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> mol of helium at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi></math>. What will be the value of the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1.2**

**Detailed Solution:**

The internal energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> moles of an ideal gas at temperature T is given by

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">nRT</mtext></math>     (<math> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math> degrees of freedom)

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>f</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>f</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><msub><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Here, <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math> = degrees of freedom of He = 3

and, <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>  = degrees of freedom of H<sub>2</sub> = 5

</details>

---

### Q13. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an energy recycling process, <math> <mrow> <mtext>X</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>g</mtext></mrow> </math> of steam at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100 </mn><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> becomes water at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100 </mn><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> which converts <math> <mrow> <mtext>Y</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>g</mtext></mrow> </math> of ice at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0 </mn><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> into water at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100 </mn><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The ratio of <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> will be (specific heat of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, specific latent heat of fusion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3.36</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, specific latent heat of vaporization <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>22.68</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Heat loss = Heat gain

<!-- MathType@Translator@5@5@MathML2 (namespace attr).tdl@MathML 2.0 (namespace attr)@ --><math > <mrow>  <msub>   <mtext>m</mtext>   <mtext>1</mtext>  </msub>  <msub>   <mtext>L</mtext>   <mtext>v</mtext>  </msub>  <mo>=</mo><msub>   <mtext>m</mtext>   <mtext>2</mtext>  </msub>  <msub>   <mrow>    <mtext>.L</mtext></mrow>   <mtext>f</mtext>  </msub>  <mo>+</mo><msub>   <mtext>m</mtext>   <mtext>2</mtext>  </msub>  <mtext>.S</mtext><mtext>.</mtext><mi>&#x0394;</mi><mtext>T</mtext></mrow></math><!-- MathType@End@5@5@ -->

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>22.68</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math> <math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>3.36</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>4200</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>100</mn></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7.56</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>22.68</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q14. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Initially, a beaker has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. Later another <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>600</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> was poured into the beaker. The temperature, <math><mi>T</mi></math> of the water (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>) after mixing is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **30**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given,

Mass of water at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>90</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>

Mass of water at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>

From calorimetry

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>90</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>120</mn></mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>210</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

</details>

### Q15. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The molar heat capacity of rock salt at low temperatures varies with temperature according to Debye's law; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1940</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>281</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The heat required to raise the temperature of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> moles of rock salt from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>800</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>373</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>273</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>273</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> ,

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mrow><msubsup><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></msubsup><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1940</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>50</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>281</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>272.79</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>273</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q16. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Liquid helium is stored at its boiling point (4.2 K) in a spherical can, separated by a vacuum space from a surrounding shield which is maintained at the temperature of liquid nitrogen (77 K). If the can is 0.1m in radius and is blacked on the outside so that it acts as a black body, how much helium boils away per hour?
(Latent heat of vapourization is 21 kJ/kg)

**Options:**

- **(A)** 43 g/h
- **(B)** 43 kg/h
- **(C)** 4.3 g/h
- **(D)** 43 x 10<sup>-3</sup> g/h

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 43 g/h**

**Detailed Solution:**

Heat absorbed per second by liquid helium <math> <mrow> <mi>&alpha;</mi> <mtext>A</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>4</mn> </msubsup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

Heat required to boil liquid helium away <math><mrow><mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dm</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dt</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>L</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dm</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dt</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&sigma;</mi> <mtext>A</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> <mn>4</mn> </msubsup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>L</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

       <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>6.67</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mtext>3.14</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>0.1</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> <msup> <mn>7</mn> <mn>4</mn> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>4.2</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

       <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1.19</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>kg/s</mo> </mrow> </math>

       <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>4.3</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>kg/h</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mo>g/h</mo> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q17. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal monatomic gas undergoes an adiabatic expansion in which its volume becomes eight times its initial value. If the initial temperature of the gas is <math> <mrow> <mn>100</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>K</mtext></mrow> </math> and the universal gas constant <math><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8.0</mn><mi>&#160;J</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>mo</mn><msup><mrow><mn>l</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mn>&#160;K</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, then how much is the decrease in its internal energy (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>) ?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **900**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>V</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>V</mi></math>

For adiabatic process <math><mo>{</mo><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mn>5/</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow></math> for monoatomic gas<math><mo>}</mo></math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>&#8901;</mo><msubsup> <mi>V</mi> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>&#8901;</mo><msubsup> <mi>V</mi> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </msubsup> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mi>V</mi></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>K</mn></math>

Loss in internal energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>f</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q18. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of ice at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is added to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of water initially at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> in a calorimeter of unknown water equivalent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi></math>. If the final temperature of the mixture is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi></math> is [Take latent heat of fusion of ice <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cal</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and specific heat capacity of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>cal</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The principle of calorimetry states that for an isolated system,</p><p>The heat gained by the cold body (Here it is ice) = The heat lost by the hot body (Here it is water + calorimeter)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>ice</mi></msub><msub><mi>L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>ice</mi></msub><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>water</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>200</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>30</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>480</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>240</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>W</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q19. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Two ideal monoatomic and diatomic gases are mixed with one another to form an ideal gas mixture. The equation of the adiabatic process of the mixture is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>PV</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math> If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are the number of moles of the monoatomic and diatomic gases in the mixture respectively, find the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">γ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>11</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>

</details>

### Q20. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two cylinders <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> fitted with pistons contain equal amounts of an ideal diatomic gas at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The piston of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is free to move, while that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is held fixed. The same amount of heat is given to the gas in each cylinder. If the rise in temperature of the gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>, then the rise in temperature of the gas in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo> </mo></math>is.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn><mo>K</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn><mo>K</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is free to move; therefore, heat will be supplied at constant pressure.

      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mtext>P</mtext></msub><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub></math>            ...(I)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is held fixed, therefore, heat will be supplied at constant volume.

           <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mtext>V</mtext></msub><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></math>            ...(ii)

But      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub></math>

     <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">nC</mtext><mtext>P</mtext></msub><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">&#8710;</mtext><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">nC</mtext><mtext>v</mtext></msub><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">&#8710;</mtext><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></msub></math>

          <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mtext>C</mtext><mtext>P</mtext></msub><msub><mtext>C</mtext><mtext>V</mtext></msub></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub></math>

          <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>    (<math> <mrow> <mi>&#947;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>1.4</mtext>&#160;</mrow></math>(diatomic) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

          <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>1.4</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>K</mo> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn><mo>K</mo></math>

</details>

### Q21. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal diatomic gas undergoes a thermodynamic process as shown in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram. The process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> is isochoric while the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BC</mi></math> is isothermal. The total heat given to the gas in the process is nearly (use <math><mi mathvariant="script">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>0.7</mn></math> )

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image11-1fc341aa-8923-412e-aa46-0e7db358b7b0.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1.4</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3.9</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1.1 </mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>3.9</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">f</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">R</mi><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">T</mi><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">f</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image12-fcaadcf6-9e43-46dd-8a52-55ae1da2e664.jpg" />

Process BC

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>∆</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="script">l</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1.4</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">e</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">A</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">B</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">C</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3.9</mn><mi mathvariant="italic"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

</details>
