### Q45. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

What is the amount of heat required (in calories) to convert <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of ice at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mo>℃</mo></math> into steam at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo> </mo><mo>℃</mo></math>? Given that latent heat of vaporization of water is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>540</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cal</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, latent heat of fusion of ice is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cal</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, the specific heat capacity of water and ice are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cal</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>  and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cal</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7250**

**Detailed Solution:**

10 g of ice at <math><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>℃</mi></math> to ice at 0<math><mi>℃</mi></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn></math> cal

10 g of ice 0<math><mi>℃</mi></math> to water at 0<math><mi>℃</mi></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>80</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mi> </mi></math>cal

10 g of water at 0<math><mi>℃</mi></math> to water at 100<math><mi>℃</mi></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn></math> cal

10 g water at 100<math><mi>℃</mi></math> to steam at 100<math><mi>℃</mi></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>540</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5400</mn></math> cal

Total heat required, <math><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mi> </mi></math>

= 50+800+1000+5400 = 7250 cal

</details>

### Q46. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two bodies of equal masses are heated at a uniform rate under identical conditions. The change in temperature in the two cases is shown graphically. The ratio of their specific heats in the solid state is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/Phys_13.JPG" style="width: 280px; height: 137px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1:3
- **(B)** 1:2
- **(C)** 2:1
- **(D)** 1:4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 1:3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>s</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>P</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>60</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>40</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>s</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>s</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><mfrac bevelled="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q47. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A diatomic ideal gas is heated at constant volume until the pressure is doubled and again heated at constant pressure until the volume is doubled. The average molar heat capacity for the whole process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>9</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>7</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>9</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let initial pressure, volume and temperature be P<sub>0</sub>, V<sub>0</sub> and T<sub>0</sub>, respectively, indicated by state A in P-V diagram. The gas is then isochorically taken to state B (2P<sub>0</sub>, V<sub>0</sub>, 2T<sub>0</sub>) and then taken from state B to state C (2P<sub>0</sub>, 2V<sub>0</sub>, 4T<sub>0</sub>) isobarically.


             <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029739_Ans..jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 237px; height: 195px;" />


Total heat absorbed by 1 mol of gas

          <math> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>v</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mtext>C</mtext> <mtext>P</mtext> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

                  <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

Total change in temperature from series A to C is <math> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

Therefore,

Molar heat capacity <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>Δ</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q48. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Steam at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is passed into <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> of water contained in a calorimeter of water equivalent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> till the temperature of the calorimeter and its contents rise to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo> </mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> . The mass of the steam condensed in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>kg</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>130</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>065</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>260</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>200</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>130</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>80</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1.1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>80</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>0.02</mn><mo>×</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>80</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mn>540</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>71.5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1.30</mn></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.130</mn></math>

</details>

### Q49. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A monatomic ideal gas sample is given heat <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math>. One half of this heat is used as work done by the gas and rest is used for increasing its internal energy. The equation of process in terms of volume and temperature is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>VT</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo></math> constant
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo></math> constant

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo></math> constant**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>PdV</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mi>dT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mi>nRT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mi>dV</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></msub><mi>dT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mi>dV</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mi>dT</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>CT</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is a constant.</p>

</details>

---

### Q50. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Corresponding to the process shown in figure, the heat given to the gas in the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. Find value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/anjana.PNG" style="width: 390px; height: 230px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cycle is clockwise. Hence heat will be given to the gas, as work done will be positive.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo></math> area of cycle = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q51. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of a diatomic gas undergoes a process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mo>[</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>]</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> are constants. The translational kinetic energy of the gas when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>35</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> <mo>/</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Therefore, translational kinetic energy is equal to

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>RT</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msub> <mtext>P</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q52. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of an ideal diatomic gas is taken through a process whose <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> diagram is shown in the figure. The work done by the gas is

<img height="117" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ebsiaoyqzguripolstvhnkfkpaoarcdpqlsjqzzbwhvmpdvuxo.jpeg" width="212" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#x3C0;P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done = Area under the curve

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q53. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<math><mi>P</mi></math> - <math><mi>V</mi></math> diagram of an ideal gas is shown. The gas undergoes from initial state <math> <mi>A</mi> </math> to final state <math> <mi>B</mi> </math> such that initial and final volumes are same. Select the correct alternative for given process <math> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow> </math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/335280q.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 161px; height: 131px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** Work done by gas is positive
- **(B)** Work done by gas is negative
- **(C)** Temperature of gas increases continuously
- **(D)** Process is isochoric

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Work done by gas is negative**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/335280s.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 142px; height: 125px;" />

Volume does not remain constant throughout the process <math> <mrow>  <mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></math>. As <math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>, temperature decreases initially as both <math> <mi>P</mi></math> and <math> <mi>V</mi></math> decreases. By area under curve, net work done is negative.

</details>

### Q54. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Some ideal monoatomic gas <math> <mtext>A</mtext> </math> in an enclosure has a pressure <math><mtext>P</mtext></math> and the temperature <math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext></mrow></math>. Another ideal monoatomic gas <math> <mtext>B</mtext> </math> enclosed in a container of the same volume has a pressure <math><mn>2</mn><mtext>P</mtext></math> and the temperature <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>. The ratio of the average kinetic energy per molecule of gas <math> <mtext>A</mtext> </math> to gas <math> <mtext>B</mtext> </math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The average kinetic energy,  <math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> 

 <math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>∝</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> 

 <math><mo>∴</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> 

 <math><mo>∴</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

</details>

### Q55. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A piece of ice falls from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> so that it melts completely. Only one-quarter of the heat produced is absorbed by the ice and all energy of ice gets converted into heat during its fall. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> is:
(Latent heat of ice is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3.4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mtext> </mtext><msup><mtext>J kg</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mtext> </mtext><msup><mtext>N kg</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>34</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>km</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>544</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>km</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>136</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>km</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>68</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>km</mtext></mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mtext>136</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>km</mtext></mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>3.4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>13.6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>136</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>136</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

### Q56. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If 2 moles of diatomic gas and 1 mole of monatomic gas are mixed, then the ratio of specific heats for the mixture is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>19</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>13</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>15</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>19</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>19</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>13</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>, </mtext> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math> (for monatomic gas) and <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>,</mtext> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>7</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </math> (for diatomic gas).

Form formula

   <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mtext>mixture</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&mu;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&gamma;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

              <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q57. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A metal sphere of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> and specific heat <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> is rotated about an axis passing through its centre, at a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> rotations per second. It is suddenly stopped and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mo>%</mo></math> of its energy is used in increasing its temperature. Then, the rise in temperature of the sphere is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>S</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>n</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi>n</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>I</mi><msup><mi>ω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Δ</mi><mi>θ</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Δ</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfrac><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>S</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q58. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Temperature and volume curves are drawn for two thermodynamic processes. For the first process, it is a straight line and for the second, it is a rectangular hyperbola. The ratio of work done in the first process to the work done in the second process is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013421_Question_1.jpg" style="width: 350px; height: 141px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2:1
- **(B)** 3:1
- **(C)** 4:1
- **(D)** 3:2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2:1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013421_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="width: 140px; height: 120px;" />
The first process is a constant pressure process, hence
w<sub>1</sub> = nR(2T<sub>0</sub> - T<sub>0</sub>) = nRT<sub>0</sub>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0013421_AnsExp_2.jpg" style="width: 140px; height: 129px;" />
The equation for the second process is
<math><mrow><mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>c</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>
Hence, <math> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>nRT</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>nRc</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>∴    </mo> <mtext>w</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <munderover> <mo>∫</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </munderover> <mtext>PdV</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>nRT</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴   </mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>w</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>w</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2 </mn> <mtext>: </mtext> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>
∴        2 : 1

</details>

### Q59. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A diatomic molecule can be modelled as two rigid balls connected with a spring such that the balls can vibrate with respect to the centre of mass of the system (spring + balls). Consider a diatomic gas made of such diatomic molecules. If the gas performs <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> of work under isobaric condition, then heat given to the gas (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>) is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **70**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>nC</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>nR</mi><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>20</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"> <mrow> <mo>=70</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>J</mtext></mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q60. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A lead bullet strikes a target with a velocity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>480</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>ms</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. After the impact, the bullet falls dead and the heat produced in the process gets equally shared between the bullet and the target. If the specific heat capacity of lead is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.03</mo><mo> </mo><mi>cal</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>, then the rise in temperature of the bullet is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 557<math><mi>℃</mi></math>
- **(B)** 457<math><mi>℃</mi></math>
- **(C)** 857<math><mi>℃</mi></math>
- **(D)** 754<math><mi>℃</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 457<math><mi>℃</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mo>∆</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></mfenced><mo>⇒</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>c</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>∆</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>J</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>∆</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>J</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>480</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>480</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>4.2</mn><mo>×</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0.03</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>457</mn><mi>℃</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q61. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A cylindrical adiabatic container of total volume <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><msub> <mi>V</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>  divided into two equal parts by a conducting piston which is free to move as shown in figure. Each part  containing identical gas at pressure <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>P</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>. Initially temperature of left and right part is <math> <mrow> <mn>4</mn><msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>  and <math> <mrow> <msub> <mi>T</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>  respectively. An external force is applied on the piston of area '<math> <mi>A</mi> </math>' to keep the piston at rest. The value of external force required when thermal equilibrium is reached is.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0629611.jpg" style="width: 249px; height: 130px;" /></p><p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>8</mn>   <mn>5</mn>  </mfrac>  <msub>   <mi>P</mi>   <mn>0</mn>  </msub>  <mi>A</mi></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>2</mn>   <mn>5</mn>  </mfrac>  <msub>   <mi>P</mi>   <mn>0</mn>  </msub>  <mi>A</mi></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>5</mn>   <mn>6</mn>  </mfrac>  <msub>   <mi>P</mi>   <mn>0</mn>  </msub>  <mi>A</mi></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>6</mn>   <mn>5</mn>  </mfrac>  <msub>   <mi>P</mi>   <mn>0</mn>  </msub>  <mi>A</mi></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math> <mrow>  <mfrac>   <mn>6</mn>   <mn>5</mn>  </mfrac>  <msub>   <mi>P</mi>   <mn>0</mn>  </msub>  <mi>A</mi></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>If final temp is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Final pressure</p><p>In left <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>In right <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>A</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q62. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Earth receives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> of solar power. If all the solar energy falling on a lens of area <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> is focussed on to a block of ice of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>280</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>, then what is the time (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>min</mi></math>) taken by the ice to melt completely? [<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi>fusion</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>105</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5.5**

**Detailed Solution:**

Heat required to melt the block

= (0.28kg) (3.3 x 10<sup>5</sup> J/kg)= 9. 24 x 10<sup>4</sup>J

Heat received per second = (1400J/ m<sup>2</sup> ) (0. 2m<sup>2</sup> ) = 280 J

Time taken (in minutes) =<math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mn>9.24</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>280</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>60</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>  = 5. 5

</details>

---

### Q63. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas expands isothermally from a volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and then compressed to original volume <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> adiabatically. The initial pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and final pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>. The total work done is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi></math>. Then:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the graph it is clear that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> .
Since the area under adiabatic process <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is greater than that of the isothermal process <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> . Therefore net word done
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>A</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/bf8690b9-59e2-48b0-8772-05ff4a5040a6.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width:2.07774in;height:1.70755in" />

</details>

---

### Q64. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Two identical systems, with heat capacity at a constant volume that varies as <math><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> (where <math><mi>b</mi></math> is a constant) are thermally isolated. Initially, one system is at a temperature <math><mn>100</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>K</mi></math> and the other is at <math><mn>200</mn><mi>K</mi></math> . The systems are then brought to thermal contact and the combined system is allowed to reach thermal equilibrium. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> then what will be the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is the final temperature in kelvin?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8.5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Energy given by a system = Energy taken by another system

<math><mi>d</mi><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi><msub><mrow><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>200</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>200</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mi>b</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>200</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>200</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>17</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>171</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q65. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A gas mixture consists of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> moles of oxygen and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> moles of argon at temperature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math>. Neglecting all vibrational modes we calculated the total internal energy of the system to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></math>. What is the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **11**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The internal energy of n moles of a gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">nFRT</mtext></math>
where F = number of degrees of freedom.
The internal energy of 2 moles of oxygen at temperature T is

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">U</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext></math>    (F = 5 for oxygen molecule)

The total internal energy of 4 moles of argon at temperature T is

    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext></math> (F = 3 for argon molecule)

Total internal energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>1</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">RT</mtext></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q66. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A cooking vessel on a slow burner contains 5 kg of water and an unknown mass of ice in equilibrium at 0<sup>o</sup>C at time t = 0. The temperature of the mixture is measured at various times and the result is plotted as shown in diagram. During the first 50 min the mixture remains at 0<sup>o</sup>C. From 50 min to 60 min, the temperature increases to 2<sup>o</sup>C. Neglecting the heat capacity of the heat capacity of the vessel, the initial mass of the ice is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0029871_Question.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 216px; height: 182px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kg</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kg</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kg</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kg</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kg</mtext> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let m be the mass of ice.
Rate of heat given by the burner is constant.
In the first 50 min

          <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dQ</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dt</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mL</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>t</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>m kg</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>&times;</mo> <mtext>4.2</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>J/kg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo> min</mo> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>               ...(i)

From 50 min to 60 min

    <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dQ</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dt</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <msub> <mtext>S</mtext> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> </mrow> </msub> <mo>&Delta;</mo> <mi>&theta;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>t</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

         <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>4.2</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>J/kg</mtext> <mo>&times;</mo> <msup> <mn>2</mn> <mo>∘</mo> </msup> <mtext>C</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo> min</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>               ...(ii)

From Eqs. (i) and (ii)

     <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

     <math> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mo>&rArr;</mo> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>kg</mtext> <mo>≃</mo> <mtext>0.7</mtext> <mo>kg</mo> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q67. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An ideal gas is taken through the cycle  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>A</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>B</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>C</mtext><mo>→</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, as shown in the figure. If the net heat supplied to the gas in the cycle is <math> <mrow> <mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext>J</mtext></mrow></math>, the work done by the gas in the process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>C</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>A</mtext></math>  is
<span style="line-height: 1.6em;">   </span><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0006066_Ques1_1.jpg" style="line-height: 1.6em; width: 287px; height: 228px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext> J</mtext></mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>10</mn><mtext>&#x00A0;J</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>15</mn><mtext>&#x00A0;J</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>20</mn><mtext>&#x00A0;J</mtext></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext> J</mtext></mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mtext>W</mtext> <mtext>AB</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>p</mn> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>V</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>10 J</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#916;</mo><msub><mtext>W</mtext><mtext>BC</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mtext>as V = constant</mtext></mfenced></math>

From first law of thermodynamics,

<math>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160; <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>W</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>U</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>U</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0 &#160; &#160; &#160; &#160;</mn> </mrow> </math> 

Process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABCA</mi></math> is cyclic,

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo>&#160; <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>AB</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>BC</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>CA</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo>&#160; <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>CA</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mtext>Q</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <msub> <mn>W</mn> <mn>AB</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mn>W</mn> <mn>BC</mn> </mrow> </math>

                  <math><mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>5 J</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q68. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One mole of a monoatomic ideal gas undergoes the process <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>⟶</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, as in the given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram. The specific heat for this process is


<img alt="" height="156" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image1-67d0f115-9220-4087-92cb-02cd662289cb.jpg" width="226" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>30</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>W</mi></math> (From <math><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup></math> law)

Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>

Temperature at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>

At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>

So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>14</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>   [Area under <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></math> diagram]

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>30</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>14</mn><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>

</details>
